    RESTRICTED. bDissEMINATION OF RESTRICTED MATTER.

    The information contained in restricted documents and the essential character-

    istics of restricted materiel may be given to any person known to be in the

    service of the United States and ta persons of undoubted loyalty and discretion

and Filter. F-2/GG, is published for the information and guidance of all concerned.

                DESTRUCTION NOTICE

WHY = 10 prevent the enemy from using or salvaging this equipment for his
             benefit.

WHEN — When ordered by your commander.

HOW —1. Smash —Use sledges, axes, handaxes, pickaxes, hammers, crowbars,
                                 heavy tools.

   2. Cut —Use axes, handaxes, machetes.

   3. Burn  —Use gasoline, kerosene, oil, flame throwers, incendiary gre-
               nades.

   4, Explosives — Use firearms, grenades, TNT.

   5. Disposal  —Bury in slit trenches, fox holes, other holes. Throw in
                    streams. Scatter.

   USE ANYTHING IMMEDIATELY AVAILABLE FOR DESTRUCTION
                                 OF THIS EQUIPMENT.

WHAT —1. Smash —Meters, controls, panels.

   2. Cut —Cables and all wiring.

   3. Burn —Resistors, capacitors, all technical manuals, instruction

x               books, tube charts.

   4. Bury or scatter—Any or all of the above pieces after destroying their
                                   usefulness.

                DESTROY EVERYTHING

         SAFETY NOTICE

VOLTAGES USED IN THIS EQUIPMENT ARE HIGH ENOUGH TO ENDANGER LIFE AND
MAY BE FATAL IF CONTACTED BY OPERATING PERSONNEL. OPERATORS MUST BE
CAREFUL NOT TO CONTACT HIGH-VOLTAGE PLATE CIRCUITS OR 115-VOLT, A-C INPUT
CONNECTIONS WHILE CHECKING OR SERVICING EQUIPMENT. MAKE CERTAIN THAT
POWER IS TURNED OFF WHEN DISASSEMBLING ANY PART OF THE EQUIPMENT.

   c. Telegraph Terminal TH-1/TCC-1’ will           terminal to the telegraph station and differen-
normally provide a satisfactory transmission        tial transmission from the telegraph station to
medium for teletypewriter signals up to 66          the telegraph terminal are used.
words per minute over any line whose attenua- -
tion does not exceed 50 db. Provision is made
for increasing the output level of the telegraph
terminal oscillator from —3 dbm to +4 dbm,
thus making it possible to operate the telegraph
terminal over a line having an extreme attenua-
tion of 57 db. To compensate for varying line
conditions and consequent variation in line
losses, an automatic volume control is pro-
vided which enables operation over circuits
having variations in attenuation of +10 db
without readjusting the set.

   d. For signaling over lines equipped with
Telegraph Terminal TH-1/TCC-1, d-c current,
20-cycle ringing, or voice-frequency signaling
may be used. A voice-frequency ringer is in-
cluded as part of the telegraph terminal and
provides for 1,000— or 500-cycle signals in-
terrupted at 19 cycles per second for lines
